|  |  |  |  |
| Modelling conventions |  |  |  |
| 1. | Fund: $20bn committed, drawn in five equal 20% instalments at the start of Years 1–5 (Prep Q3). | Fund: $20bn committed, drawn in five equal 20% instalments at the start of Years 1–5 (Prep Q3). | Fund: $20bn committed, drawn in five equal 20% instalments at the start of Years 1–5 (Prep Q3). |
| 2. | Management fee = fee rate × committed capital, paid at the start of every year (Years 1–10) out of drawn capital / fund assets, exactly as the template does: Assets + Takedown − Fee = Invested capital. | Management fee = fee rate × committed capital, paid at the start of every year (Years 1–10) out of drawn capital / fund assets, exactly as the template does: Assets + Takedown − Fee = Invested capital. | Management fee = fee rate × committed capital, paid at the start of every year (Years 1–10) out of drawn capital / fund assets, exactly as the template does: Assets + Takedown − Fee = Invested capital. |
| 3. | Invested capital grows 20% during the year. From the end of Year 5, 20% of NAV is distributed each year; everything left is liquidated at the end of Year 10. | Invested capital grows 20% during the year. From the end of Year 5, 20% of NAV is distributed each year; everything left is liquidated at the end of Year 10. | Invested capital grows 20% during the year. From the end of Year 5, 20% of NAV is distributed each year; everything left is liquidated at the end of Year 10. |
| 4. | Whole-fund waterfall: (1) LPs get back all contributed capital, including the part used for fees; (2) preferred return, if any; (3) GP catch-up, soft hurdle only; (4) the rest split 80/20 (or 85/15). | Whole-fund waterfall: (1) LPs get back all contributed capital, including the part used for fees; (2) preferred return, if any; (3) GP catch-up, soft hurdle only; (4) the rest split 80/20 (or 85/15). | Whole-fund waterfall: (1) LPs get back all contributed capital, including the part used for fees; (2) preferred return, if any; (3) GP catch-up, soft hurdle only; (4) the rest split 80/20 (or 85/15). |
| 5. | Hurdle = 8% a year compounded on unreturned capital and unpaid preferred return, i.e. LPs must earn an 8% IRR before carry. Hard: carry only on profits above that. Soft: the GP takes 100% of distributions after the hurdle until it has 20% of all profits. | Hurdle = 8% a year compounded on unreturned capital and unpaid preferred return, i.e. LPs must earn an 8% IRR before carry. Hard: carry only on profits above that. Soft: the GP takes 100% of distributions after the hurdle until it has 20% of all profits. | Hurdle = 8% a year compounded on unreturned capital and unpaid preferred return, i.e. LPs must earn an 8% IRR before carry. Hard: carry only on profits above that. Soft: the GP takes 100% of distributions after the hurdle until it has 20% of all profits. |
| 6. | Timing: in the cash-flow rows each column is a point in time t; the start of Year n is t = n − 1. IRR and NPV use LP net cash flows; GP PV uses fees (start of year) and carry (end of year), all at 15%. | Timing: in the cash-flow rows each column is a point in time t; the start of Year n is t = n − 1. IRR and NPV use LP net cash flows; GP PV uses fees (start of year) and carry (end of year), all at 15%. | Timing: in the cash-flow rows each column is a point in time t; the start of Year n is t = n − 1. IRR and NPV use LP net cash flows; GP PV uses fees (start of year) and carry (end of year), all at 15%. |
| 7. | TPG's own ~$500m commitment is ignored (all capital is treated as LP capital). OPERF gets 750 ÷ 20,000 = 3.75% of LP cash flows. | TPG's own ~$500m commitment is ignored (all capital is treated as LP capital). OPERF gets 750 ÷ 20,000 = 3.75% of LP cash flows. | TPG's own ~$500m commitment is ignored (all capital is treated as LP capital). OPERF gets 750 ÷ 20,000 = 3.75% of LP cash flows. |
| 8. | TPG's 30% cap on 2009 capital calls is not binding in this model (draws are 20% a year). | TPG's 30% cap on 2009 capital calls is not binding in this model (draws are 20% a year). | TPG's 30% cap on 2009 capital calls is not binding in this model (draws are 20% a year). |

| On top of the offer, an 8% hard hurdle adds 66 bps and a 15% carry adds 55 bps; a soft hurdle (with catch-up) adds only 9 bps at 20% growth. |  |  |  |  |  |

| F. Talking points (update with the model) |  |  |  |  |  |
| The fee cut costs TPG about $30m a year on TPG VI; even if every LP cut 10%, fee income falls by $56m a year — roughly 8% of TPG's firm-wide fee base. |  |  |  |  |  |
| 2008 fundraising was a record $314bn but the number of funds closed fell 20%; IPO proceeds collapsed 86% and TPG VI lost $437m on WaMu (2.2% of the fund). |  |  |  |  |  |
| Under current terms 48% of TPG's compensation (PV) is fees it receives in every outcome; carry does not start until Year 8. |  |  |  |  |  |
| LP satisfaction fell from 76% to 56% in eight months; 57% of LPs named management fees as needing improvement, only 12% named the hurdle. |  |  |  |  |  |

| D. Context from the case |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| •  TPG typically offered a preferred return on its funds; other OPERF GPs (e.g. KKR) had resisted including one (case p.6, fn 16). |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| •  An 80/20 profit split is the industry standard (case p.4); Exhibit 5 shows carry of 20–30% of gains — 15% would be below market. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| •  LP survey, Dec 2008 (Exhibit 11): areas requiring improvement — management fees 57%, carry structure & clawback 37%, hurdle 12%. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| •  LP satisfaction with the compensation structure fell from 76% to 56% (Apr–Dec 2008); strongly dissatisfied rose from 13% to 27% (case p.6, Exhibit 11). |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| •  ILPA principles (Exhibit 12), endorsed by the Oregon Investment Council: the agreed profit split has "typically worked well" — the push is on fees, clawbacks and GP commitment. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
